── what this run does and does not show ────────────
All native fixtures used here carry the composited material (materialRendered: true).
the shape axis is BOUNDED to the declared component region (margin 0 device px): within it, coverage, contour and corner profile are measured as before; outside it nothing is recovered, so a surface drawn LARGER than its declaration reads as a match. Area recovery is assumed, not measured — read silhouetteArea{Native,Web} against componentRegionArea.
shadow axis NOT NORMALISED on 5 cell(s) across 5 scene(s): dark-solid__capsule-button__rest-tint-orange, impulse__capsule-button__rest-tint-orange, dark-solid__capsule-button__inactive-tint-orange, impulse__capsule-button__inactive-tint-orange, dark-solid__capsule-button__rest-tint-blue. Those backdrops carry too little light for an occlusion ratio to exist; the absolute departure is still reported and every ratio is absent, not zero.
caveat: Profiles not captured because the system is in accessibility mode 'standard' and these claim another: apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-reduced-transparency, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-increased-contrast. macOS exposes reduce-transparency and increase-contrast as read-only environment values, so each such profile needs its own run with the corresponding System Settings toggle on (Accessibility > Display).
caveat: Profiles not captured because this display renders at 2.0x and their keys state another scale: apple-macos-26.5-1x-dark-standard, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard. A profile is only captured on a display whose backingScaleFactor matches its key — a mismatched run would file 640x400-pixel fixtures under a key claiming a different scale, with no trace in the bytes.

── what this run does and does not show ────────────
All native fixtures used here carry the composited material (materialRendered: true).
the shape axis is BOUNDED to the declared component region (margin 0 device px): within it, coverage, contour and corner profile are measured as before; outside it nothing is recovered, so a surface drawn LARGER than its declaration reads as a match. Area recovery is assumed, not measured — read silhouetteArea{Native,Web} against componentRegionArea.
shadow axis NOT NORMALISED on 5 cell(s) across 5 scene(s): dark-solid__capsule-button__rest-tint-orange, impulse__capsule-button__rest-tint-orange, dark-solid__capsule-button__inactive-tint-orange, impulse__capsule-button__inactive-tint-orange, dark-solid__capsule-button__rest-tint-blue. Those backdrops carry too little light for an occlusion ratio to exist; the absolute departure is still reported and every ratio is absent, not zero.
coherence axis on 41 of 41 dom cell(s) — this tier against its texture twin, web against web.
caveat: Profiles not captured because the system is in accessibility mode 'standard' and these claim another: apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-reduced-transparency, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-increased-contrast. macOS exposes reduce-transparency and increase-contrast as read-only environment values, so each such profile needs its own run with the corresponding System Settings toggle on (Accessibility > Display).
caveat: Profiles not captured because this display renders at 2.0x and their keys state another scale: apple-macos-26.5-1x-dark-standard, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard. A profile is only captured on a display whose backingScaleFactor matches its key — a mismatched run would file 640x400-pixel fixtures under a key claiming a different scale, with no trace in the bytes.
web accessibility for apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard (native standard): browser preferences, no overrides

── what this run does and does not show ────────────
All native fixtures used here carry the composited material (materialRendered: true).
the shape axis is BOUNDED to the declared component region (margin 0 device px): within it, coverage, contour and corner profile are measured as before; outside it nothing is recovered, so a surface drawn LARGER than its declaration reads as a match. Area recovery is assumed, not measured — read silhouetteArea{Native,Web} against componentRegionArea.
shadow axis NOT NORMALISED on 5 cell(s) across 5 scene(s): impulse__capsule-button__inactive-tint-orange, dark-solid__capsule-button__rest-tint-blue, impulse__capsule-button__rest-tint-orange, dark-solid__capsule-button__inactive-tint-orange, dark-solid__capsule-button__rest-tint-orange. Those backdrops carry too little light for an occlusion ratio to exist; the absolute departure is still reported and every ratio is absent, not zero.
caveat: Profiles not captured because the system is in accessibility mode 'standard' and these claim another: apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-reduced-transparency, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-increased-contrast. macOS exposes reduce-transparency and increase-contrast as read-only environment values, so each such profile needs its own run with the corresponding System Settings toggle on (Accessibility > Display).
caveat: Profiles not captured because this display renders at 2.0x and their keys state another scale: apple-macos-26.5-1x-dark-standard, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard. A profile is only captured on a display whose backingScaleFactor matches its key — a mismatched run would file 640x400-pixel fixtures under a key claiming a different scale, with no trace in the bytes.
web accessibility for apple-macos-26.5-2x-light-standard (native standard): browser preferences, no overrides

── what this run does and does not show ────────────
All native fixtures used here carry the composited material (materialRendered: true).
the shape axis is BOUNDED to the declared component region (margin 0 device px): within it, coverage, contour and corner profile are measured as before; outside it nothing is recovered, so a surface drawn LARGER than its declaration reads as a match. Area recovery is assumed, not measured — read silhouetteArea{Native,Web} against componentRegionArea.
shadow axis NOT NORMALISED on 5 cell(s) across 5 scene(s): impulse__capsule-button__inactive-tint-orange, dark-solid__capsule-button__rest-tint-blue, impulse__capsule-button__rest-tint-orange, dark-solid__capsule-button__inactive-tint-orange, dark-solid__capsule-button__rest-tint-orange. Those backdrops carry too little light for an occlusion ratio to exist; the absolute departure is still reported and every ratio is absent, not zero.
coherence axis on 41 of 41 dom cell(s) — this tier against its texture twin, web against web.
caveat: Profiles not captured because the system is in accessibility mode 'standard' and these claim another: apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-reduced-transparency, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-increased-contrast. macOS exposes reduce-transparency and increase-contrast as read-only environment values, so each such profile needs its own run with the corresponding System Settings toggle on (Accessibility > Display).
caveat: Profiles not captured because this display renders at 2.0x and their keys state another scale: apple-macos-26.5-1x-dark-standard, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard. A profile is only captured on a display whose backingScaleFactor matches its key — a mismatched run would file 640x400-pixel fixtures under a key claiming a different scale, with no trace in the bytes.
web accessibility for apple-macos-26.5-2x-light-standard (native standard): browser preferences, no overrides

── what this run does and does not show ────────────
All native fixtures used here carry the composited material (materialRendered: true).
the shape axis is BOUNDED to the declared component region (margin 0 device px): within it, coverage, contour and corner profile are measured as before; outside it nothing is recovered, so a surface drawn LARGER than its declaration reads as a match. Area recovery is assumed, not measured — read silhouetteArea{Native,Web} against componentRegionArea.
shadow axis NOT NORMALISED on 2 cell(s) across 2 scene(s): dark-solid__capsule-button__rest-tint-orange, dark-solid__capsule-button__inactive-tint-orange. Those backdrops carry too little light for an occlusion ratio to exist; the absolute departure is still reported and every ratio is absent, not zero.
caveat: Profiles not captured because the system is in accessibility mode 'standard' and these claim another: apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-reduced-transparency, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-increased-contrast. macOS exposes reduce-transparency and increase-contrast as read-only environment values, so each such profile needs its own run with the corresponding System Settings toggle on (Accessibility > Display).
caveat: Profiles not captured because this display renders at 2.0x and their keys state another scale: apple-macos-26.5-1x-dark-standard, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard. A profile is only captured on a display whose backingScaleFactor matches its key — a mismatched run would file 640x400-pixel fixtures under a key claiming a different scale, with no trace in the bytes.
web accessibility for apple-macos-26.5-1x-dark-standard (native standard): browser preferences, no overrides

── what this run does and does not show ────────────
All native fixtures used here carry the composited material (materialRendered: true).
the shape axis is BOUNDED to the declared component region (margin 0 device px): within it, coverage, contour and corner profile are measured as before; outside it nothing is recovered, so a surface drawn LARGER than its declaration reads as a match. Area recovery is assumed, not measured — read silhouetteArea{Native,Web} against componentRegionArea.
shadow axis NOT NORMALISED on 2 cell(s) across 2 scene(s): dark-solid__capsule-button__rest-tint-orange, dark-solid__capsule-button__inactive-tint-orange. Those backdrops carry too little light for an occlusion ratio to exist; the absolute departure is still reported and every ratio is absent, not zero.
coherence axis on 20 of 20 dom cell(s) — this tier against its texture twin, web against web.
caveat: Profiles not captured because the system is in accessibility mode 'standard' and these claim another: apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-reduced-transparency, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-increased-contrast. macOS exposes reduce-transparency and increase-contrast as read-only environment values, so each such profile needs its own run with the corresponding System Settings toggle on (Accessibility > Display).
caveat: Profiles not captured because this display renders at 2.0x and their keys state another scale: apple-macos-26.5-1x-dark-standard, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard. A profile is only captured on a display whose backingScaleFactor matches its key — a mismatched run would file 640x400-pixel fixtures under a key claiming a different scale, with no trace in the bytes.
web accessibility for apple-macos-26.5-1x-dark-standard (native standard): browser preferences, no overrides

── what this run does and does not show ────────────
All native fixtures used here carry the composited material (materialRendered: true).
the shape axis is BOUNDED to the declared component region (margin 0 device px): within it, coverage, contour and corner profile are measured as before; outside it nothing is recovered, so a surface drawn LARGER than its declaration reads as a match. Area recovery is assumed, not measured — read silhouetteArea{Native,Web} against componentRegionArea.
shadow axis NOT NORMALISED on 2 cell(s) across 2 scene(s): dark-solid__capsule-button__inactive-tint-orange, dark-solid__capsule-button__rest-tint-orange. Those backdrops carry too little light for an occlusion ratio to exist; the absolute departure is still reported and every ratio is absent, not zero.
caveat: Profiles not captured because the system is in accessibility mode 'standard' and these claim another: apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-reduced-transparency, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-increased-contrast. macOS exposes reduce-transparency and increase-contrast as read-only environment values, so each such profile needs its own run with the corresponding System Settings toggle on (Accessibility > Display).
caveat: Profiles not captured because this display renders at 2.0x and their keys state another scale: apple-macos-26.5-1x-dark-standard, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard. A profile is only captured on a display whose backingScaleFactor matches its key — a mismatched run would file 640x400-pixel fixtures under a key claiming a different scale, with no trace in the bytes.
web accessibility for apple-macos-26.5-2x-dark-standard (native standard): browser preferences, no overrides

── what this run does and does not show ────────────
All native fixtures used here carry the composited material (materialRendered: true).
the shape axis is BOUNDED to the declared component region (margin 0 device px): within it, coverage, contour and corner profile are measured as before; outside it nothing is recovered, so a surface drawn LARGER than its declaration reads as a match. Area recovery is assumed, not measured — read silhouetteArea{Native,Web} against componentRegionArea.
shadow axis NOT NORMALISED on 2 cell(s) across 2 scene(s): dark-solid__capsule-button__inactive-tint-orange, dark-solid__capsule-button__rest-tint-orange. Those backdrops carry too little light for an occlusion ratio to exist; the absolute departure is still reported and every ratio is absent, not zero.
coherence axis on 20 of 20 dom cell(s) — this tier against its texture twin, web against web.
caveat: Profiles not captured because the system is in accessibility mode 'standard' and these claim another: apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-reduced-transparency, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-increased-contrast. macOS exposes reduce-transparency and increase-contrast as read-only environment values, so each such profile needs its own run with the corresponding System Settings toggle on (Accessibility > Display).
caveat: Profiles not captured because this display renders at 2.0x and their keys state another scale: apple-macos-26.5-1x-dark-standard, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard. A profile is only captured on a display whose backingScaleFactor matches its key — a mismatched run would file 640x400-pixel fixtures under a key claiming a different scale, with no trace in the bytes.
web accessibility for apple-macos-26.5-2x-dark-standard (native standard): browser preferences, no overrides

── what this run does and does not show ────────────
All native fixtures used here carry the composited material (materialRendered: true).
the shape axis is BOUNDED to the declared component region (margin 0 device px): within it, coverage, contour and corner profile are measured as before; outside it nothing is recovered, so a surface drawn LARGER than its declaration reads as a match. Area recovery is assumed, not measured — read silhouetteArea{Native,Web} against componentRegionArea.
caveat: Profiles not captured because the system is in accessibility mode 'standard' and these claim another: apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-reduced-transparency, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-increased-contrast. macOS exposes reduce-transparency and increase-contrast as read-only environment values, so each such profile needs its own run with the corresponding System Settings toggle on (Accessibility > Display).
caveat: Profiles not captured because this display renders at 2.0x and their keys state another scale: apple-macos-26.5-1x-dark-standard, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard. A profile is only captured on a display whose backingScaleFactor matches its key — a mismatched run would file 640x400-pixel fixtures under a key claiming a different scale, with no trace in the bytes.
web accessibility for apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-reduced-transparency (native reduced-transparency): reduced-transparency

── what this run does and does not show ────────────
All native fixtures used here carry the composited material (materialRendered: true).
the shape axis is BOUNDED to the declared component region (margin 0 device px): within it, coverage, contour and corner profile are measured as before; outside it nothing is recovered, so a surface drawn LARGER than its declaration reads as a match. Area recovery is assumed, not measured — read silhouetteArea{Native,Web} against componentRegionArea.
coherence axis on 9 of 9 dom cell(s) — this tier against its texture twin, web against web.
caveat: Profiles not captured because the system is in accessibility mode 'standard' and these claim another: apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-reduced-transparency, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-increased-contrast. macOS exposes reduce-transparency and increase-contrast as read-only environment values, so each such profile needs its own run with the corresponding System Settings toggle on (Accessibility > Display).
caveat: Profiles not captured because this display renders at 2.0x and their keys state another scale: apple-macos-26.5-1x-dark-standard, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard. A profile is only captured on a display whose backingScaleFactor matches its key — a mismatched run would file 640x400-pixel fixtures under a key claiming a different scale, with no trace in the bytes.

── what this run does and does not show ────────────
All native fixtures used here carry the composited material (materialRendered: true).
the shape axis is BOUNDED to the declared component region (margin 0 device px): within it, coverage, contour and corner profile are measured as before; outside it nothing is recovered, so a surface drawn LARGER than its declaration reads as a match. Area recovery is assumed, not measured — read silhouetteArea{Native,Web} against componentRegionArea.
coherence axis on 11 of 11 dom cell(s) — this tier against its texture twin, web against web.
caveat: Profiles not captured because the system is in accessibility mode 'standard' and these claim another: apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-reduced-transparency, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-increased-contrast. macOS exposes reduce-transparency and increase-contrast as read-only environment values, so each such profile needs its own run with the corresponding System Settings toggle on (Accessibility > Display).
caveat: Profiles not captured because this display renders at 2.0x and their keys state another scale: apple-macos-26.5-1x-dark-standard, apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-standard. A profile is only captured on a display whose backingScaleFactor matches its key — a mismatched run would file 640x400-pixel fixtures under a key claiming a different scale, with no trace in the bytes.
web accessibility for apple-macos-26.5-1x-light-increased-contrast (native increased-contrast): reduced-transparency + increased-contrast
